Let me break it down: ### What is the "National Median Income"? - The **median income** is the income level at which half of the population earns more, and half earns less. - It’s not the same as the **average income**, which is calculated by adding up all incomes and dividing by the number of earners. ### What does it mean to be in the "50% of the national median"?

If you’re earning at the median income level, you’re exactly in the middle.

Being at or near the 50% mark (median) means: - > **User (2025-01-05 20:57:38 UTC):** > > Are you considered to be more poor at 50% less than the average medium income or 25% or even 30% median?

There's no telling what, exactly, occurred.

Maybe, like us, you still do. > > We have spoken to many others who have corroborated this uncanny deviation in the population's cumulative attitude.

Like some bizarre new COVID variant, it literally materialized overnight, and, since then, has only grown worse. > > At first, we thought it was us, that we had allowed our own shared opinion of society, which was already quite poor, to become infected with a bleakness and cynicism that even we didn't know was within us. > > However, it became more and more apparent over time that the sudden contentious nature of the people around us was real, not just a product of our stressed out minds, and any need for contemplation was swiftly negated, anyway on the ninth of June, 2023, when absolute proof of this notion was driven home for us, with an abundance of overkill, only a few months after we'd first begun to sense negativity's pervasive arrival. > > It honestly appears that the government wants us to simply die off.

It seems like the Powers That Be have found a surreptitious solution for the homeless problem.

This is certainly a bold statement, so please allow me to provide a few examples to alleviate any skepticism. > > Let's start with food stamps.

The Department Of Children And Families allows EBT recipients to purchase any food item in the entire grocery store, with only one stark exception: you're not allowed to purchase hot foods with EBT benefits.

Well, that one rule is a doozy, as it eliminates almost everything healthy, nutritious, or fresh from your grocery list.

I will concede that canned foods and cooking ingredients are included, but these require a knowledge of and knack for meal preparation that I just don't possess, and, even though Janina is actually a good cook, our tent didn't come with an oven or a microwave, and the businesses around us go to great lengths to deny us the use of even their outdoor power outlets. > > The end result of this is that I now weigh a mere 155 pounds, which is less than I was in the ninth grade.

I am obviously malnourished, and many of the other homeless people we encounter are equally gaunt. > > The moratorium on hot food which DCF has chosen to enforce upon us has created an inadvertent army of gaunt zombies, weakened and shriveled by malnutrition, and no matter how much I've wracked my brain to figure out a logical reason for this rule, I have yet to come up with one that makes any sense, especially considering that after Hurricane Ian, they were able to make hot food available temporarily. > > The realization that DCF could make hot food available with such ease whenever they chose to was nothing less than an open-handed slap in the face. > > We are, understandably, more eager than ever to know the true reasons behind their conscious choice to deprive us of the kind of sustenance we need to stay strong here in our own personal Phantom Zone. > > We once lived in a place and time that was a Utopia in comparison to where we are now, but of course we didn't know that then.

We never know when it actually matters.

It only becomes clear through the polarizing filter of regret. > > One night almost a year back, I was walking along SW 137th Avenue in Homestead, oblivious to any danger while listening to my headphones, when I suddenly found myself doing a Barani flip through the air.

My comprehension of what had just happened came at the zenith of my arc as gravity took hold and I began my plunge.

By the time I landed, hard, in the middle of the street, I was cognizant enough ofmy predicament to get up as fast as I could before another car finished the first one's job.

Only two days prior, we'd been rudely uprooted from our previous home in the Family Dollar parking lot and had been forced to have the vehicle we were living in towed to an empty field across the street. > > It was the only place left for us to go. > > Utterly oblivious to any danger, we were falling asleep while watching Netflix in our Cadillac, when, absolutely out of nowhere, the owner of the house behind our car ambushed us with a torrent of violence.

All of them, even the ACLU, have failed to respond as of today, 02/23/24. > > Perhaps it was naive of us – and we really have no business being naive at this point – but we really expected to finally be taken seriously.

In our minds, these were the ones who should have been on our side.

Without them, we have no recourse. > > We are seriously beginning to wonder if this buck ever stops.

Every individual, regardless of their socioeconomic status, deserves dignity and protection from harm. > > "Woke Culture" is nothing but a mask everyone has put on in a collective effort to convey the illusion that our society is finally, after decades of struggle, free of all bigotry…but that's simply not the case.

We know from personal experience that hate is yet here among us.

As a survivor of a heinous and senseless hate crime, I stand as a testament to the plight of the homeless, whose voices are drowned out by the cacophony of indifference and neglect.

In an era supposedly marked by inclusivity, the reality for the homeless is a stark contradiction.

Around a year ago, a seismic shift occurred in the collective attitude towards the homeless—a shift so profound and pervasive that its origins remain shrouded in mystery.

Like a virus, it spread overnight, infecting hearts and minds with prejudice and contempt.

Initially, we questioned ourselves, attributing the hostility to our own perceptions.

Yet, as time passed, the reality of our plight became undeniable, culminating in a brutal attack on June 9th, 2023—a harrowing reminder of society's indifference to our suffering.

It seems that the government has turned a blind eye to our plight, opting for a covert solution to the homeless problem.

The systemic neglect is evident in policies such as the restriction on hot food purchases using EBT benefits—a rule that deprives us of vital nutrition and sustenance.

The consequences are dire.

The tragedy of Tony Cordero's death serves as a grim reminder of the dangers we face daily.

Struck down by a semi-truck on SW 137th Avenue, Tony's gruesome demise highlights the absence of basic safety measures, such as sidewalks and bike lanes, on perilous thoroughfares.

The horrors we endure extend beyond physical violence.

The apathy of authorities, exemplified by the dismissal of our pleas for assistance, perpetuates a cycle of neglect and despair.

In the face of institutional indifference, we refuse to remain silent.

I implore the media to heed our call, to amplify our voices, and to stand with us in our fight against injustice.

Together, we can create a society where every individual is valued and respected, regardless of their circumstances.

Sincerely, [Your Name] > **User (2024-03-07 22:36:34 UTC):** > > Please give me the last media message where we left off **Assistant (2024-03-07 22:37:00 UTC):** Certainly, here's the last media message where we left off: --- **Homestead, FL, 02/23/24** – As a homeless person and the survivor of a heinous and senseless hate crime, I am compelled to bring attention to an appalling problem in our society which is being systematically neglected, and people are getting hurt, and even killed, as a result.

In an era supposedly marked by inclusivity, the reality for the homeless is a stark contradiction.

The shift happened around a year or so ago.

There's no telling what, exactly, occurred.

Whatever it was, it was massive, and its reach hopelessly far.

Perhaps you felt it, too.

Maybe, like us, you still do.

We have spoken to many others who have corroborated this uncanny deviation in the population's cumulative attitude.

Like some bizarre new COVID variant, it literally materialized overnight, and, since then, has only grown worse.

At first, we thought it was us, that we had allowed our own shared opinion of society, which was already quite poor, to become infected with a bleakness and cynicism that even we didn't know was within us.

However, it became more and more apparent over time that the sudden contentious nature of the people around us was real, not just a product of our stressed out minds, and any need for contemplation was swiftly negated, anyway on the ninth of June, 2023, when absolute proof of this notion was driven home for us, with an abundance of overkill, only a few months after we'd first begun to sense negativity's pervasive arrival.

It honestly appears that the government wants us to simply die off.

It seems like the Powers That Be have found a surreptitious solution for the homeless problem.

This is certainly a bold statement, so please allow me to provide a few examples to alleviate any skepticism.

Let's start with food stamps.

The Department Of Children And Families allows EBT recipients to purchase any food item in the entire grocery store, with only one stark exception: you're not allowed to purchase hot foods with EBT benefits.

Well, that one rule is a doozy, as it eliminates almost everything healthy, nutritious, or fresh from your grocery list.

I will concede that canned foods and cooking ingredients are included, but these require a knowledge of and knack for meal preparation that I just don't possess, and, even though Janina is actually a good cook, our tent didn't come with an oven or a microwave, and the businesses around us go to great lengths to deny us the use of even their outdoor power outlets.

The end result of this is that I now weigh a mere 155 pounds, which is less than I was in the ninth grade.

I am obviously malnourished, and many of the other homeless people we encounter are equally gaunt.

The moratorium on hot food which DCF has chosen to enforce upon us has created an inadvertent army of gaunt zombies, weakened and shriveled by malnutrition, and no matter how much I've wracked my brain to figure out a logical reason for this rule, I have yet to come up with one that makes any sense, especially considering that after Hurricane Ian, they were able to make hot food available temporarily.

The realization that DCF could make hot food available with such ease whenever they chose to was nothing less than an open-handed slap in the face.

We are, understandably, more eager than ever to know the true reasons behind their conscious choice to deprive us of the kind of sustenance we need to stay strong here in our own personal Phantom Zone.

We once lived in a place and time that was a Utopia in comparison to where we are now, but of course we didn't know that then.

We never know when it actually matters.

It only becomes clear through the polarizing filter of regret.

One night almost a year back, I was walking along SW 137th Avenue in Homestead, oblivious to any danger while listening to my headphones, when I suddenly found myself doing a Barani flip through the air.

My comprehension of what had just happened came at the zenith of my arc as gravity took hold and I began my plunge.

By the time I landed, hard, in the middle of the street, I was cognizant enough ofmy predicament to get up as fast as I could before another car finished the first one's job.

I stood up fully expecting my shattered legs to buckle underneath me, and I knew immediately, upon finding myself surprisingly intact, that I'd been divinely spared.

The driver, who didn't even see him, drove right over Tony without even knowing it.

There was no real reason for me to know that, and we didn't find out that it was him officially until four days later.

It was a Saturday night, at about a quarter to ten.

Only two days prior, we'd been rudely uprooted from our previous home in the Family Dollar parking lot and had been forced to have the vehicle we were living in towed to an empty field across the street.

It was the only place left for us to go.

Utterly oblivious to any danger, we were falling asleep while watching Netflix in our Cadillac, when, absolutely out of nowhere, the owner of the house behind our car ambushed us with a torrent of violence.

Wielding a canister of pepper spray that I didn't notice until it was already too late, he launched a vicious physical assault on me, my fiance, and even our registered service dog, unwittingly igniting a harrowing sequence of events that ended up serving to epitomize the neglect and prejudice we face daily.

The indifference of authorities was quite palpable.

The extent of their apathy was driven home when, over the course of the next seven months, the neighbor proceeded to menace us on twenty more occasions.

Yes, you read that right.

All of them, even the ACLU, have failed to respond as of today, 02/23/24.

Perhaps it was naive of us – and we really have no business being naive at this point – but we really expected to finally be taken seriously.

In our minds, these were the ones who should have been on our side.

Without them, we have no recourse.

We are seriously beginning to wonder if this buck ever stops.

To think that this dismissive attitude continues even farther up the chain than we'd considered is a bleak notion indeed, and highlights just how badly we need your help.

Every individual, regardless of their socioeconomic status, deserves dignity and protection from harm. "Woke Culture" is nothing but a mask everyone has put on in a collective effort to convey the illusion that our society is finally, after decades of struggle, free of all bigotry…but that's simply not the case.

We know from personal experience that hate is yet here among us.

It's just playing a sinister game of hide and seek, and it's thriving in the dark like a fungus.

I implore you, the media, to heed our pleas, to amplify our voices, and to stand with us in our fight against injustice.

Together, we can create a society where every individual is valued and respected, regardless of their circumstances.

For further information and inquiries, please contact Joshua Durden and Janina Miranda Lafitte at (561) 862-9957.
